Invoice extraction: original pairs and same-input repetitions

Same-PDF batch follow-up (2 new pairs): 1/2 baseline packets correct and 2/2 guided packets correct. Calls per run: [36, 68] → [12, 16]; total calls: 104 → 28.

Original three-packet comparison: Fully correct packets: 3/3 baseline → 3/3 guided. Exact item rows: 48 → 48. Tool calls: 52 → 53.

Task model: aws/claude-sonnet-4-5. Reflection model: aws/claude-sonnet-4-5. Claude Code 2.1.287, Cortex, full Evolve, Poppler and Tesseract run in one Docker container; model inference runs through the configured API. The author-side oracle is absent from the task container.

Architecture

Task and inputs

Each packet has 16 line items across three synthetic invoices: a text PDF; a two-page text PDF with repeated headings and a description continuing across the page boundary; and a PDF with a text first page and an image-only second page. IDs, vendors, descriptions and amounts differ between training and each evaluation packet. Credits use parentheses; one unit price has three decimal places, with its rounded line amount printed. One invoice per packet has a deliberately mismatched printed total.

Extract every invoice line item from the PDFs in input/ into items.csv.
Use exactly these columns: invoice_id,vendor,description,quantity,unit_price,line_amount.
Preserve invoice IDs and complete item descriptions, including continuation text.
Include credits and discounts as signed line items. Do not include table headings,
page subtotals, tax, or grand totals as items. Preserve exact decimal amounts.

Reconcile each invoice's sum of line amounts plus its printed tax against its
printed total. Write reconciliation.csv with exactly these columns:
invoice_id,subtotal,tax,printed_total,computed_total,difference,status.
Difference means computed_total minus printed_total. Status must be matched
when the difference is zero and discrepancy otherwise. Report any discrepancy.

Keep all source PDFs unchanged. Use the installed local tools; do not install
packages or write persistent memory files. If using Python, run it with
uv run --offline --no-sync --project /opt/demo python.
Show the successful commands and briefly explain how you checked the result.

Results

PairEntire packet correctExact item rowsExact reconciliationsTool callsTool errorsHarnessMatched comparison
1True → True16/16 → 16/163/3 → 3/316 → 153 → 2interactive → interactiveTrue
2True → True16/16 → 16/163/3 → 3/317 → 184 → 4interactive → interactive (transition)False
3True → True16/16 → 16/163/3 → 3/319 → 202 → 3noninteractive → noninteractiveTrue
1-repeat-2True → True16/16 → 16/163/3 → 3/336 → 124 → 1noninteractive → noninteractiveTrue
1-repeat-3False → True14/16 → 16/163/3 → 3/368 → 165 → 3noninteractive → noninteractiveTrue

Rows are checked against author-side reference fields, preserving IDs and complete descriptions and comparing decimals exactly. Whitespace and numeric formatting are normalized; duplicate and extra rows count against correctness. Each arm receives identical PDFs, prompt and tools, a fresh UUID, and a separate Claude configuration/workspace. Only training supplies learning and feedback. Reviewed text is frozen before evaluation. Elapsed time includes approvals and interaction and is excluded from efficiency comparisons.

Repeated identical inputs

The original three pairs use different packets. Same-input repetitions, when present, use the original PDFs and prompt in fresh configurations and workspaces. Repetition 1 refers to the original pair and appears in both summaries; do not count it twice. This is exploratory follow-up, not a pre-registered confirmatory study. The table records every session's harness, and the separate noninteractive subset excludes changed-harness pairs.

Harness and interruptions

Sessions with a harness change: baseline-2. Interrupted recordings are preserved, and the same UUID is resumed with a neutral continuation request. Those pairs are excluded from matched-harness claims. Noninteractive sessions use --print, --allowedTools Bash,Read,Write,Edit, --permission-mode dontAsk and --permission-prompts none. No stdin is used; unapproved operations are denied. Both arms retain the same task model and tools. The original three-packet totals above include all observed pairs and are descriptive. The per-session launch history records neutral continuation prompts and permission flags.

Generated guidelines

Candidate 1 · promoted unchanged

When reading files in a task, always verify the working directory first and construct absolute paths from it. Avoid assuming relative paths will resolve correctly.

Candidate 2 · not promoted

For structured data extraction from PDFs, manually extract data by reading the PDFs directly rather than attempting automated parsing libraries, especially when working in constrained environments with limited package installation.

## Verified Extraction and Checking Procedure Summary

### Successful Approach

**1. PDF Reading and Data Extraction:**
- Used the `Read` tool to examine all 3 PDFs (001071.pdf, 001082.pdf, 001093.pdf)
- Manually extracted invoice data from the PDF text content, carefully preserving:
  - Complete multi-line descriptions with continuation text (invoice 001082's seal kit description spanned pages)
  - Signed negative values for credits/discounts (-9.80, -35.00, -25.00)
  - Exact decimal precision (2.5, 4.375, 1245.60)
  - Vendor names from invoice headers

**2. CSV Generation:**
- Created Python script that directly encoded the extracted data
- Used `uv run --offline --no-sync --project /opt/demo python` as required (no package installation)
- Generated **items.csv** with exact column specification: invoice_id, vendor, description, quantity, unit_price, line_amount
- Excluded all subtotals, tax rows, and grand totals as instructed

**3. Reconciliation:**
- Used Python's `Decimal` type for precise financial calculations
- Computed totals as: subtotal (sum of line amounts) + tax
- Compared against printed totals
- Generated **reconciliation.csv** with difference = computed_total - printed_total
- Status logic: "matched" when difference = 0, "discrepancy" otherwise

**4. Verification:**
- Confirmed 16 line items extracted (4 from 001071, 6 from 001082, 6 from 001093)
- Detected and reported 1 discrepancy: Invoice 001093 off by -$0.01
- Verified source PDFs unchanged via checksums

### Approaches That Failed

**1. File Path Error:**
- Initial attempt to read PDFs failed because I used relative `input/` path instead of full `/results/tasks/training/input/` path
- Fixed by checking `pwd` and using absolute paths

**2. Display Formatting:**
- Attempted to use `column` command for table formatting, but it wasn't installed
- Not a critical failure—used `sed` alternative for display purposes only

### Key Success Factors

- Manual data extraction from PDF text avoided OCR/parsing complexity
- Decimal arithmetic prevented floating-point rounding errors in reconciliation
- Exact adherence to column names and CSV structure
- Proper handling of negative values for credits/discounts
- Complete description preservation including continuation text across pages

All 16 items and 3 reconciliation rows confirmed to match reference data.

Remove commas\\n    amount_str = amount_str.replace(',', '')\\n    # Check if it's in parentheses (credit/negative)\\n    if amount_str.startswith('(') and amount_str.endswith(')'):\\n        return Decimal(amount_str[1:-1]) * -1\\n    return Decimal(amount_str)\\n\\ndef extract_invoice_data(pdf_path):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Extract line items and totals from an invoice PDF\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    reader = pypdf.PdfReader(pdf_path)\\n    \\n    # Extract text from all pages\\n    full_text = \\\"\\\"\\n    for page in reader.pages:\\n        full_text += page.extract_text() + \\\"\\\\n\\\"\\n    \\n    # Extract invoice ID\\n    invoice_match = re.search(r'INVOICE\\\\s+(\\\\d+)', full_text)\\n    invoice_id = invoice_match.group(1) if invoice_match else \\\"\\\"\\n    \\n    # Extract vendor name (first line)\\n    lines = full_text.split('\\\\n')\\n    vendor = lines[0].strip() if lines else \\\"\\\"\\n    \\n    # Extract line items - looking for pattern: Description Qty Unit_price Amount\\n    line_items = []\\n    \\n    # Split text into lines and process\\n    in_items_section = False\\n    current_description = \\\"\\\"\\n    \\n    for i, line in enumerate(lines):\\n        line = line.strip()\\n        \\n        # Skip empty lines\\n        if not line:\\n            continue\\n            \\n        # Check if we've reached the description header\\n        if line.startswith(\\\"Description\\\") and i+1 < len(lines) and \\\"Qty\\\" in line:\\n            in_items_section = True\\n            continue\\n        \\n        # Skip non-item lines\\n        if not in_items_section:\\n            continue\\n            \\n        # Try to match a line item pattern\\n        # Pattern: description followed by numbers for qty, unit_price, amount\\n        # Amount is always at the end, may be in parentheses\\n        \\n        # Check if this line ends with an amount (number or (number))\\n        amount_pattern = r'(\\\\([\\\\d,]+\\\\.[\\\\d]{2}\\\\)|[\\\\d,]+\\\\.[\\\\d]{2})$'\\n        amount_match = re.search(amount_pattern, line)\\n        \\n        if amount_match:\\n            amount_str = amount_match.group(1)\\n            # Extract the part before the amount\\n            line_before_amount = line[:amount_match.start()].strip()\\n            \\n            # Try to extract unit price (second-to-last number)\\n            unit_price_pattern = r'(\\\\([\\\\d,]+\\\\.[\\\\d]{2,3}\\\\)|[\\\\d,]+\\\\.[\\\\d]{2,3})\\\\s+' + re.escape(amount_str) + r'$'\\n            full_match = re.search(unit_price_pattern, line)\\n            \\n            if full_match:\\n                unit_price_str = full_match.group(1)\\n                line_before_price = line[:full_match.start()].strip()\\n                \\n                # Extract quantity (third-to-last number/value)\\n                qty_pattern = r'([\\\\d.]+)\\\\s+' + re.escape(unit_price_str)\\n                qty_match = re.search(qty_pattern, line)\\n                \\n                if qty_match:\\n                    qty_str = qty_match.group(1)\\n                    description = line[:qty_match.start()].strip()\\n                    \\n                    # No unit price found, might be part of description\\n                current_description = line\\n        else:\\n            # No amount at end, this is likely a continuation of description\\n            if current_description:\\n                current_description += \\\" \\\" + line\\n            else:\\n                current_description = line\\n    \\n    # import csv
import re
from decimal import Decimal
import pypdf

def parse_amount(amount_str):
    """Parse amount string, handling parentheses for negative values"""
    amount_str = amount_str.strip()
    # Remove commas
    amount_str = amount_str.replace(',', '')
    # Check if it's in parentheses (credit/negative)
    if amount_str.startswith('(') and amount_str.endswith(')'):
        return Decimal(amount_str[1:-1]) * -1
    return Decimal(amount_str)

def extract_invoice_data(pdf_path):
    """Extract line items and totals from an invoice PDF"""
    reader = pypdf.PdfReader(pdf_path)
    
    # Extract text from all pages
    full_text = ""
    for page in reader.pages:
        full_text += page.extract_text() + "\n"
    
    # Extract invoice ID
    invoice_match = re.search(r'INVOICE\s+(\d+)', full_text)
    invoice_id = invoice_match.group(1) if invoice_match else ""
    
    # Extract vendor name (first line)
    lines = full_text.split('\n')
    vendor = lines[0].strip() if lines else ""
    
    # Extract line items - looking for pattern: Description Qty Unit_price Amount
    line_items = []
    
    # Split text into lines and process
    in_items_section = False
    current_description = ""
    
    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
        line = line.strip()
        
        # Skip empty lines
        if not line:
            continue
            
        # Check if we've reached the description header
        if line.startswith("Description") and i+1 < len(lines) and "Qty" in line:
            in_items_section = True
            continue
        
        # Stop at subtotal/tax/total lines
        if line.startswith("Subtotal") or line.startswith("Tax") or line.startswith("Total due"):
            break
            
        # Skip continuation messages
        if "Invoice continues" in line or "Totals are on" in line:
            continue
            
        # Skip non-item lines
        if not in_items_section:
            continue
            
        # Try to match a line item pattern
        # Pattern: description followed by numbers for qty, unit_price, amount
        # Amount is always at the end, may be in parentheses
        
        # Check if this line ends with an amount (number or (number))
        amount_pattern = r'(\([\d,]+\.[\d]{2}\)|[\d,]+\.[\d]{2})$'
        amount_match = re.search(amount_pattern, line)
        
        if amount_match:
            amount_str = amount_match.group(1)
            # Extract the part before the amount
            line_before_amount = line[:amount_match.start()].strip()
            
            # Try to extract unit price (second-to-last number)
            unit_price_pattern = r'(\([\d,]+\.[\d]{2,3}\)|[\d,]+\.[\d]{2,3})\s+' + re.escape(amount_str) + r'$'
            full_match = re.search(unit_price_pattern, line)
            
            if full_match:
                unit_price_str = full_match.group(1)
                line_before_price = line[:full_match.start()].strip()
                
                # Extract quantity (third-to-last number/value)
                qty_pattern = r'([\d.]+)\s+' + re.escape(unit_price_str)
                qty_match = re.search(qty_pattern, line)
                
                if qty_match:
                    qty_str = qty_match.group(1)
                    description = line[:qty_match.start()].strip()
                    
                    # If we have accumulated description from previous lines, prepend it
                    if current_description:
                        description = current_description + " " + description
                        current_description = ""
                    
                    try:
                        qty = Decimal(qty_str)
                        unit_price = parse_amount(unit_price_str)
                        amount = parse_amount(amount_str)
                        
                        line_items.append({
                            'invoice_id': invoice_id,
                            'vendor': vendor,
                            'description': description,
                            'quantity': qty,
                            'unit_price': unit_price,
                            'line_amount': amount
                        })
                    except:
                        # If parsing fails, this might be a continuation line
                        current_description = line
                else:
                    # No quantity found, might be part of description
                    current_description = line
            else:
                # No unit price found, might be part of description
                current_description = line
        else:
            # No amount at end, this is likely a continuation of description
            if current_description:
                current_description += " " + line
            else:
                current_description = line
    
    # Extract totals
    subtotal_match = re.search(r'Subtotal\s+([\d,]+\.[\d]{2})', full_text)
    tax_match = re.search(r'Tax\s+([\d,]+\.[\d]{2})', full_text)
    total_match = re.search(r'Total due\s+([\d,]+\.[\d]{2})', full_text)
    
    subtotal = parse_amount(subtotal_match.group(1)) if subtotal_match else Decimal('0')
    tax = parse_amount(tax_match.group(1)) if tax_match else Decimal('0')
    total = parse_amount(total_match.group(1)) if total_match else Decimal('0')
    
    return line_items, subtotal, tax, total

# Process all PDFs
import os
all_items = []
reconciliation = []

pdf_files = sorted([f for f in os.listdir('input') if f.endswith('.pdf')])

for pdf_file in pdf_files:
    pdf_path = os.path.join('input', pdf_file)
    print(f"Processing {pdf_file}...")
    
    line_items, printed_subtotal, tax, printed_total = extract_invoice_data(pdf_path)
    
    # Add items to list
    all_items.extend(line_items)
    
    # Calculate computed subtotal (sum of line amounts)
    computed_subtotal = sum(item['line_amount'] for item in line_items)
    computed_total = computed_subtotal + tax
    difference = computed_total - printed_total
    
    # Determine status
    status = "matched" if difference == 0 else "discrepancy"
    
    if line_items:
        invoice_id = line_items[0]['invoice_id']
        reconciliation.append({
            'invoice_id': invoice_id,
            'subtotal': computed_subtotal,
            'tax': tax,
            'printed_total': printed_total,
            'computed_total': computed_total,
            'difference': difference,
            'status': status
        })
        
        print(f"  Invoice {invoice_id}: {len(line_items)} items, status={status}")
        if status == "discrepancy":
            print(f"    WARNING: Difference of {difference}")

# Write items.csv
with open('items.csv', 'w', newline='') as f:
    writer = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=['invoice_id', 'vendor', 'description', 'quantity', 'unit_price', 'line_amount'])
    writer.writeheader()
    writer.writerows(all_items)

print(f"\nWrote {len(all_items)} line items to items.csv")

# Write reconciliation.csv
with open('reconciliation.csv', 'w', newline='') as f:
    writer = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=['invoice_id', 'subtotal', 'tax', 'printed_total', 'computed_total', 'difference', 'status'])
    writer.writeheader()
    writer.writerows(reconciliation)

print(f"Wrote {len(reconciliation)} reconciliation records to reconciliation.csv")

# Report any discrepancies
discrepancies = [r for r in reconciliation if r['status'] == 'discrepancy']
if discrepancies:
    print(f"\n⚠️  FOUND {len(discrepancies)} DISCREPANCIES:")
    for d in discrepancies:
        print(f"  Invoice {d['invoice_id']}: difference = {d['difference']}")
else:
    print("\n✓ All invoices reconciled successfully!")

Ah! I see the issue now. On page 2, there's another "Description Qty Unit price Amount" header, and after that we have "compatible with..." as continuation lines, then "Precision shims" as a new item. The problem is that the continuation logic treats "compatible with..." as part of the next item.

The key insight: when we see lines without numbers (like "compatible with..."), they should only be continuations if we're currently accumulating for an incomplete item. Once we hit a page break or another header, we should clear any accumulated continuations.

Let me fix this:

## Summary

I've successfully completed the task:

### **Commands Used:**
1. **`uv run --offline --no-sync --project /opt/demo python extract_invoices.py`** - Extracted all invoice data and created both CSV files using the installed Python environment

### **Results:**

**items.csv** - Contains 16 line items with columns: `invoice_id,vendor,description,quantity,unit_price,line_amount`
- Preserved all invoice IDs (002071, 002082, 002093)
- Captured complete descriptions including continuation text across pages
- Credits/discounts properly signed as negative values (-9.80, -36.00, -26.00)
- Excluded table headings, page subtotals, tax, and grand totals
- Preserved exact decimal amounts

**reconciliation.csv** - Contains 3 reconciliation records with columns: `invoice_id,subtotal,tax,printed_total,computed_total,difference,status`
- Invoices 002071 and 002082: **matched** (difference = 0.00)
- Invoice 002093: **discrepancy** (difference = -0.02)

### **Discrepancy Reported:**
⚠️ **Invoice 002093** has a $0.02 discrepancy where the computed total (775.60 + 90.64 = 866.24) is $0.02 less than the printed total (866.26).

All source PDFs remain unchanged (verified via md5sum).

Interpretation

All six original evaluation packets were fully correct: every run matched all 16 line-item rows and all 3 reconciliations. Baseline tool calls were 16, 17 and 19; guided calls were 15, 18 and 20. The original totals are 52 versus 53, with no overall efficiency gain or correctness gain. Structured tool errors totaled nine in each arm.

The two fresh noninteractive runs on identical packet-1 PDFs used [36, 68] baseline calls and [12, 16] guided calls. These small samples are exploratory; the full same-input distribution also includes the original interactive pair.

In the final same-input batch baseline, 68 tool calls still left 14/16 exact line-item rows; 3/3 reconciliations were correct. The corresponding guided run used 16 calls and produced every row correctly. The other batch pair was fully correct in both arms, with 36 baseline versus 12 guided calls. This is an observed benefit on two fresh matched-harness pairs; the original different-packet comparison remains mixed, so these observations do not establish general reliability.

Original pair 2 changed harness mid-session and is descriptive only. The two original pairs with matched harnesses used 35 calls in each arm: one guided run saved a call and the other used one more. This does not establish a repeatable benefit.

The three original baselines already used Decimal. The first same-input baseline repeat initially used floats and tried unavailable PDF libraries before switching to Decimal and rewriting the outputs; it finished correctly after 36 tool calls. Guided runs did not consistently follow the working-directory advice; some repeated guessed temporary absolute paths. The candidate advice also discourages relative paths, although valid relative paths worked in other sessions. Recorded injection proves that the reviewed guidelines were supplied, not that they caused a particular choice.

The same-input repetitions preserve the original PDFs and prompt and freeze the reviewed selection. Repetition 1 is the original interactive pair; repetitions 2 and 3 use the noninteractive harness in both arms. Read the noninteractive subset separately to avoid treating a harness difference as ordinary model variation.

Another task to test would be repeated repairs in a small unfamiliar data-import codebase. The training repair should discover concrete facts such as the actual test invocation, database reset procedure and parser failure pattern. Fresh bugs in that same component can then test whether Evolve reduces repeated investigation while preserving independent test correctness. Those facts should remain available to the baseline in the repository; this is a proposed experiment, not a measured result.

The failed 68-call baseline retained all monetary values and all three reconciliations correctly. One description lost its substantial page-continuation text, which is a material extraction error. A second exact-text mismatch was only the spacing change from 2 m to 2m. Even allowing that spacing difference, the packet still fails because the continuation is missing.

Interpretation limits

These are a small set of paired observations on synthetic invoices, with an exploratory same-input follow-up. They do not estimate performance on arbitrary vendor invoices or isolate causation from ordinary model variation. Cortex's first-request attribution establishes recorded injection; the final outbound payload is not independently inspected. Cortex stores parsed text and tool calls/results; PDF image bytes and hidden reasoning are absent from the converted trajectory. All failed outputs are retained. Tool errors count distinct Cortex isError tool-result IDs; a shell error inside an otherwise successful Bash result may not appear in that count. Each task has a fresh configuration and workspace, but the tasks share a container and results volume; filesystem permissions do not prevent access to another run. The author-side oracle is absent from that container.
